# Phase 10 O1 live pilot — research run record

Recorded by the root Codex host, model GPT-6, on 2026-09-09. This report records the live MCP procedure and saved outcomes for owner review. It assigns no T1/T5 truth rating and does not fill the O1 table in provider-port.md. All times below are UTC (local time was EDT, UTC−04:00).

## Binary, build, project, and configuration

Binary: `/code/cadence/target/debug/cadence`

SHA-256: `9cfee55120b79aae461dbcc120a9fd9eaa9a0875528d66451f9469210ab832db`

Source branch: `cadence/binary-owns-process`; HEAD: `ad24e0859985cdb356e8cf1602c8f19253603db6`. One build command was run: `RUSTC_WRAPPER= cargo build -p cadence`. Cargo reported `Finished dev profile [unoptimized + debuginfo]` in **15.05 seconds**, exit 0. Binary build artifact modification time: **2026-09-09T19:50:12.996384223Z**. The exact command start wall time was not captured; the artifact timestamp and Cargo elapsed time are the retained build-time observations. There was no second build.

Pilot project: `/tmp/claude-1000/-code-cadence/79e77d12-0bf8-40da-a15c-d917e21ca9c0/scratchpad/pilot`

Pilot commit: `7bd13d4f3a93d00be0e27b5c5084f93e269938d0`; author `John Crenshaw <john@jcrenshaw.dev>`; created with `-c commit.gpgsign=false`. The only committed pilot file is `src/example.rs`; the pilot config and run records are retained uncommitted. The resident was always started with cwd equal to the pilot project and argv `[/code/cadence/target/debug/cadence, --project-root, /tmp/claude-1000/-code-cadence/79e77d12-0bf8-40da-a15c-d917e21ca9c0/scratchpad/pilot, serve]`. No resident was pointed at `/code/cadence`.

The pilot `.planning/state.json`, `items.jsonl`, and `decisions.jsonl` remain on disk. The report was the only file written under `/code/cadence/.planning`; no cadence source was edited and no cadence commit was made.

Project configuration was supplied at `<pilot>/.planning/config.json`; the binary created `<pilot>/.planning/config.v4.json` with the same JSON values on import. Config lookup is rooted at the bound project’s `.planning` directory. The server’s default global path is `$HOME/.claude/cadence/config.json`; `CADENCE_GLOBAL_CONFIG=""` disabled that global layer for every pilot session. The provider credential-file lookup is separate from this global config layer.

Explicit project config (all keys and values):

```json
{
  "review": {
    "mode": "single",
    "reviewers": [
      "openai"
    ],
    "triggers": {
      "diff": {
        "gate": "advisory",

      }

          "cheap": "gpt-5-mini"
        }
      }

```

`review.key_file` was not explicitly set; saved effective `key_file` is null. `OPENAI_API_KEY` was absent in the inherited successful-session environment (presence-only expansion produced an empty marker), so the binary used its supported file fallback at `/home/john/.config/cadence/providers.env`. That file and its values were never printed or copied into the driver. For the deliberate failure, the second run’s host process had the prescribed deliberately invalid `OPENAI_API_KEY` override; the saved HTTP 401 shows that environment precedence reached the real endpoint. The no-credential alternative was not used. The invalid value is redacted in this report as well.

Both provider dispatches used the built-in `https://api.openai.com/v1/responses` transport. No fixture transport or endpoint override was used. The driver issued one successful native provider attempt and one deliberate 401 native attempt, with no retry of a paid request. Saved cost/currency are null; no price or billing amount is inferred.

Before the first `review-next`, the polling path was read in `crates/cadence/src/review/selection.rs` (`select_next`) and `crates/cadence/src/review_service.rs` (issued-attempt selection and `tokio::spawn` around lines 1180–1394). A saved incomplete issued attempt yields no new selection; native work is spawned only for an issued requested choice. Later pending polls reconnect to that saved attempt; a durable provider failure allows the one saved fallback choice. This was the required code-based spend check, not a test or a truth verdict.

## Restart and reread comparisons

Each host restart closed stdin, waited for process exit 0, and launched the same binary against the same pilot store. No process was killed. Comparisons below use the returned `structuredContent.result` JSON values, excluding JSON-RPC IDs and transport timestamps. Full request/response objects remain in the transcript.

| Run/readback | Before | After | Returned body equal |
|---|---|---|---|
| Success attempt | success-pre-attempt | success-post-attempt | true |
| Success original | success-pre-original | success-post-original | true |
| Success inventory | success-pre-inventory | success-post-inventory | true |
| Success terminal next | success-next-poll-2 | success-post-next | true |
| Failed provider attempt | failure-pre-provider-attempt | failure-post-provider-attempt | true |
| Local fallback attempt | failure-pre-fallback-attempt | failure-post-fallback-attempt | true |
| Local original | failure-pre-fallback-original | failure-post-fallback-original | true |
| Both-run inventory | failure-pre-inventory | failure-post-inventory | true |
| Failed-run terminal next | failure-pre-next | failure-post-next | true |
| Final inventory after both next calls | failure-post-inventory | failure-post-inventory-after-next | true |
| Success attempt across final restart | success-post-attempt | failure-post-success-attempt | true |
| Success original across final restart | success-post-original | failure-post-success-original | true |

| Inventory | Admissions | Attempts / issued | Originals | Closures | Provider evidence |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| success-pre-inventory | 1 | 1 / 1 | o1 | f1-a1 | f1-a1 |
| success-post-inventory | 1 | 1 / 1 | o1 | f1-a1 | f1-a1 |

The successful run has one issued attempt and one original. The failed run has exactly provider `f2-a1` and fallback `f2-a2`; each has one `records.closures[attempt]` member. The final inventory still has only the same two native launch/return pairs, one local launch/return pair, and originals `o1`/`o2`. Both terminal next calls return `usable-complete`; no new dispatch is present in their responses. The saved failed provider error remains unchanged through restart.

## Deviations and observation limits

1. The phase is called the 4.0.0 rewrite, but the built binary’s initialize response reports version `3.7.12`. The report records that actual version and exact binary hash; source was not changed.
2. `review-admit` returns only `{fire, attempt, replayed}`. Full admission and attempt fields used for the fallback identity were copied from `review-next`. The successful provider attempt’s view changes from `f1-v1` to its saved fenced view `f1-a1-fenced`; the local fallback uses its own returned `f2-a2-view`.
3. Pending is initially `state: pending`, then `state: delivery, delivery: pending`. Terminal next is `state: delivery` with `delivery` and `completion` equal to `usable-complete`, rather than a top-level state of `usable-complete`. The binary’s actual shape was followed.
4. `review-original` returns `identity`, `raw_bytes` (a JSON byte array), `findings`, and `record`. Those exact responses are retained below; the readable local envelope above is the unchanged host return, not a rewritten original.
5. The local routing slot is literally `claude-subagent`, with requested agent `cad-reviewer-medium`, requested model null and requested effort `medium`. Its `fallback_for` field is null in single mode. Its role as this fire’s fallback is visible through the admission selection and failed-provider-to-dispatch transition. Observed identity was honestly recorded as Codex/GPT-6. The dispatch omitted its optional model field; no Claude subagent was claimed.
6. The schema default for `review.triggers.diff.gate` is `off`; the pilot explicitly used `advisory` to admit the intended review. The binary imported project `config.json` to `config.v4.json`. Global config was disabled to isolate the pilot; supported provider-file credentials still resolved independently.
7. Build start wall time was not captured. Exact binary artifact mtime and Cargo’s 15.05-second elapsed build time are supplied instead of inventing a start timestamp.
8. This is a Codex-produced research record for the owner to inspect. Owner acceptance/O1 seen status, truth ratings, billing reconciliation, and provider finding correctness were not evaluated. No tests, plan execution, fixes, source patches, or cadence commits were performed.

## Complete redacted MCP transcript, steps 1–6

Every MCP initialize request, initialized notification, tool request, and received response is reproduced below with wall-clock timestamps, session and step. Responses preserve both text content and structuredContent. JSON is compactly formatted for large retained byte arrays and inventories; no response fields are intentionally omitted. `response: null` on the initialized notification means no reply was expected. Host starts/stops and the actual local return-production event are included.

All report content passed the credential fence before writing: credential-bearing token/password/secret/key/bearer [REDACTED] and credential-shaped strings are replaced with `[REDACTED]`. Non-secret routing identities such as `replay_key`, config path fields such as `key_file`, and numeric usage fields remain interpretable. The existing binary fence had already sanitized the provider’s error excerpt. The original retained Rust material and local findings contain no credential values. The supplemental redacted transport log and driver remain under the scratchpad; this report contains the complete transcript itself.

by zero can occur for a single-element slice.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"When samples.len() == 1, the expression (samples.len() as i64 - 1) evaluates to 0, and executing the division in Some(... / 0) causes a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"high\",\"claim\":\"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).\",\"failure_scenario\":\"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"medium\",\"claim\":\"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. causes a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"high\",\"claim\":\"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).\",\"failure_scenario\":\"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"medium\",\"claim\":\"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2."},{"file":"src/example.rs","line":7,"severity":"medium","claim":"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.","failure_scenario":"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. The function performs no overflow handling or use of a wider accumulator (e.g., i128) to mitigate this."}]},"attempt":"f1-a1","host_return":"native-response:f1-a1","artifact":"m1","view":"f1-a1-fenced","acceptance":{"state":"accepted"},"closure":{"attempt":"f1-a1","original":"o1","terminal":"accepted","acknowledged_at":1788983548},"citations":[{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":0},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"},{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":1},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"},{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":2},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"}]}},"original_sequence":1,"closures":{"f1-a1":{"attempt":"f1-a1","original":"o1","terminal":"accepted","acknowledged_at":1788983548}}},"deferred":{"members":[]}}},"isError":false}}}

causes a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"high\",\"claim\":\"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).\",\"failure_scenario\":\"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"medium\",\"claim\":\"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results."},{"file":"src/example.rs","line":7,"severity":"high","claim":"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).","failure_scenario":"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2."},{"file":"src/example.rs","line":7,"severity":"medium","claim":"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.","failure_scenario":"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. The function performs no overflow handling or use of a wider accumulator (e.g., i128) to mitigate this."}]},"attempt":"f1-a1","host_return":"native-response:f1-a1","artifact":"m1","view":"f1-a1-fenced","acceptance":{"state":"accepted"},"closure":{"attempt":"f1-a1","original":"o1","terminal":"accepted","acknowledged_at":1788983548},"citations":[{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":0},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"},{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":1},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"},{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":2},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"}]}},"original_sequence":1,"closures":{"f1-a1":{"attempt":"f1-a1","original":"o1","terminal":"accepted","acknowledged_at":1788983548}}},"deferred":{"members":[]}}},"isError":false}}}

causes a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"high\",\"claim\":\"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).\",\"failure_scenario\":\"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"medium\",\"claim\":\"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. incorrect divisor (off-by-one).","failure_scenario":"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2."},{"file":"src/example.rs","line":7,"severity":"medium","claim":"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.","failure_scenario":"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. The function performs no overflow handling or use of a wider accumulator (e.g., i128) to mitigate this."}]},"attempt":"f1-a1","host_return":"native-response:f1-a1","artifact":"m1","view":"f1-a1-fenced","acceptance":{"state":"accepted"},"closure":{"attempt":"f1-a1","original":"o1","terminal":"accepted","acknowledged_at":1788983548},"citations":[{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":0},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"},{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":1},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"},{"finding":{"original":"o1","index":2},"submitted":null,"unresolved_reason":"not-supplied"}]}},"original_sequence":1,"closures":{"f1-a1":{"attempt":"f1-a1","original":"o1","terminal":"accepted","acknowledged_at":1788983548}}},"deferred":{"members":[]}}},"isError":false}}}

by zero can occur for a single-element slice.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"When samples.len() == 1, the expression (samples.len() as i64 - 1) evaluates to 0, and executing the division in Some(... / 0) causes a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"high\",\"claim\":\"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).\",\"failure_scenario\":\"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"medium\",\"claim\":\"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. incorrect divisor (off-by-one).","failure_scenario":"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2."},{"file":"src/example.rs","line":7,"severity":"medium","claim":"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.","failure_scenario":"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. by zero can occur for a single-element slice.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"When samples.len() == 1, the expression (samples.len() as i64 - 1) evaluates to 0, and executing the division in Some(... / 0) causes a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"high\",\"claim\":\"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).\",\"failure_scenario\":\"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"medium\",\"claim\":\"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. incorrect divisor (off-by-one).","failure_scenario":"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2."},{"file":"src/example.rs","line":7,"severity":"medium","claim":"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.","failure_scenario":"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. by zero can occur for a single-element slice.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"When samples.len() == 1, the expression (samples.len() as i64 - 1) evaluates to 0, and executing the division in Some(... / 0) causes a panic (debug builds) or undefined/wrapped behaviour (release), crashing or producing incorrect results.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"high\",\"claim\":\"Mean calculation uses an incorrect divisor (off-by-one).\",\"failure_scenario\":\"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2.\"},{\"file\":\"src/example.rs\",\"line\":7,\"severity\":\"medium\",\"claim\":\"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.\",\"failure_scenario\":\"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values. In debug builds this causes a panic; in release builds it wraps, producing an incorrect mean. incorrect divisor (off-by-one).","failure_scenario":"For samples of length N > 1 the code divides the sum by (N - 1) instead of N, so the returned mean is systematically too large (sum / (N-1)) and thus incorrect for virtually all non-empty inputs. E.g., for samples [1,2,3] the function returns (6 / 2) = 3 instead of (6 / 3) = 2."},{"file":"src/example.rs","line":7,"severity":"medium","claim":"Summing i64 values may overflow before division.","failure_scenario":"samples.iter().sum::<i64>() can overflow for large or many sample values.
